#268. ¿Baena contra Cucurella, en 'La Velada' de Ibai Llanos?

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

El teatro Palladium de Londres albergó una gala del Balón de Oro que dejó episodios muy comentados fuera de los galardones oficiales.

Ibai Llanos abordó la alfombra roja británica con su micrófono para lanzar una pregunta singular a los futbolistas: ¿con qué colega de profesión se medirían en un cuadrilátero de boxeo durante La Velada del Año?

La réplica más llamativa llegó por parte de Marc Cucurella. El carrilero del Real Madrid, protagonista reciente en el Clásico por su intenso marcaje sobre Lamine Yamal en la victoria blanca por 1-2, fijó su objetivo en un futbolista del bando colchonero

El catalán se acordó de su amigo Álex Baena. "Con Baena. Es amigo mío, pero es un tío que saca lo peor de mí; le reviento", aseveró el zaguero merengue ante el comunicador vasco. El macroevento de boxeo que organiza este último acumula registros históricos en las plataformas de streaming, con una media superior a los seis millones de espectadores simultáneos en su edición de 2026.

El guante no quedó en el suelo en el plano digital. El centrocampista del Atlético de Madrid, pieza clave en el esquema rojiblanco y compañero de Cucurella en la selección española que se coronó campeona del mundo este verano, replicó a través de Instagram.

Su contestación resultó tajante: "Cuando quieras, sitio, día y hora". El cruce de declaraciones revolucionó las redes sociales de inmediato, donde la réplica del futbolista colchonero cosechó más de 50.000 apoyos en apenas un par de horas y trasladó la rivalidad de los derbis madrileños al terreno virtual.

#75. Kylian Mbappé’s China crisis shows how sensitive Mao’s legacy remains | Rebecca Bailey

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Real Madrid forward was dropped by brands for comments Chinese media generally steered clear of outlining

It’s been a bad start to the week for the Kylian Mbappé. The France forward missed out on joining the elite roster of Ballon d’Or winners and instead became the latest high-profile addition to a far less illustrious list – that of foreign brands and individuals charged with “hurting the feelings of the Chinese people”.

The saga began when a video of the Real Madrid player making light of a meme involving him and China’s former leader Mao Zedong began spreading.

Within hours Mbappé found himself condemned online, before being unceremoniously dropped by brands, then held responsible for state television suspending the entire top Spanish league from its schedule. On Tuesday Mbappé apologised – “My comment was ill-judged, and I now understand that it hurt many people in China” – as did La Liga, which released a statement saying it “deeply regrets the offence that Kylian Mbappé’s recent comments have caused among Chinese fans”.

Foreign brands from Mercedes to Versace and celebrities from John Cena to Katy Perry have previously been sent to the sin bin for crossing China’s political lines on Tibet, Hong Kong and Taiwan.

Mbappé’s case shows how sensitive Mao’s legacy remains in China 50 years after his death – to the extent that most Chinese coverage of the affair does not even mention what the footballer said.

In the video, Mbappé references a meme of himself and “a guy … I think he was a Chinese dictator”, before a composite image of Mbappé’s face on Mao’s body pops up. (Mbappé has been nicknamed “the dictator” since his time at Paris Saint-Germain, a joke referring to his perceived influence at the club.)

The football app Dongqiudi was among the first to release a statement condemning the “inappropriate remarks”, but said: “Because his comments were offensive to China and the Chinese people, we cannot translate them.” Chinese media have reported on reactions such as the brand desertions, and the China Football Association’s lodging of a “stern protest” with Real Madrid – who apologised on Wednesday “to the Chinese people for any offence caused” – but have generally steered clear of outlining Mbappé’s precise offence.

The topic was still trending on Chinese social media on Wednesday morning, with one hashtag relating to the superstar’s apology boasting more than 600m views. Some posts asked variations on a simple question: “What did he actually say?”

Manya Koetse, editor of the Eye on Digital China newsletter, said in a post on X: “Many netizens without a VPN are still left in the dark about why all the talk about boycotting Mbappé is actually happening.”

Still, many expressed anger at what was characterised as disrespect for, as well as a misunderstanding of, China’s history. “For the majority of the [Chinese] people, Mao was and is still not seen as negatively as it is believed in the west,” said Mobo Gao, professor of Chinese studies at Adelaide University.

Mao was the founding leader of modern China, but his legacy includes radical policies such as the Great Leap Forward, an industrialisation policy blamed for a famine that killed as many as 30 million people by some estimates.

The ruling Communist party (CCP) maintains Mao was a revolutionary hero, though after his death it acknowledged another extreme policy, the violent Cultural Revolution, was a “grave mistake”.

Gao said: “There has been intense tension between Mao hatred and Mao support among the Chinese themselves. This tension is even more keenly felt since the economy has slowed.”

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Some scholars have pointed to a resurgence of interest in Mao among younger Chinese, with a yearning for a time they regard as more idealistic and egalitarian – something the CCP is keen to keep in check.

Jennifer Pan, professor of communication at Stanford University, said the difficulty of doing independent social science surveys in the past decade meant “our understanding of what Chinese people actually think is weak”. Pan said: “Social media doesn’t fill that gap. It’s noisy, unrepresentative, and censored … What we see is that companies, platforms, broadcasters and the foreign ministry moved together quickly. Did this follow public anger, or did this produce public anger? Either is possible, and we can’t tell which came first.”

Whichever way round, the financial cost for Mbappé is the same. His face is a common sight on Chinese buses, in subways and in online ads, and his commercial deals could be worth “as much as $30m (£22.6m) per year”, said Mark Dreyer, author of Sporting Superpower, a book about China’s ambitions in the sector.

However, “as soon as someone else has decided you’ve crossed the line, it’s a race to who can cut ties quickest, who can be the most patriotic”, he said. “Then you have to kiss the ring – there really is no other way if you want to stay in the market.”

Mbappé’s and La Liga’s swift apologies suggest they are trying to avoid a fate like the NBA, whose lucrative pre-season matches were shut out of China for six years after the Houston Rockets’ then general manager Daryl Morey voiced support for Hong Kong pro-democracy protests in 2019.

La Liga may have better luck, with the state broadcaster CCTV saying it would listen to what Mbappé had to say, and “more importantly” watch what he does.

On Wednesday a foreign ministry spokesperson, Guo Jiakun, said: “We take seriously the apology the individual concerned has made.”

Dreyer said: “It doesn’t sound like it’s an instant ‘all is forgiven’. But it sounds like they’re going to be a little bit more, for want of a better word, reasonable.”

#56. Egypt: Watch Live - TotalEnergies CAF Champions League and TotalEnergies CAF Confederation Cup 2026/27 Group Stage Draws Today in Cairo

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Confédération Africaine de Football ("CAF") will conduct the TotalEnergies CAF Champions League and TotalEnergies CAF Confederation Cup 2026/27 Group Stage Draws today, Wednesday, 28 October in Cairo, Egypt.

Football fans can follow both draws live on CAF's official YouTube channel, CAF TV, and across CAF's official digital platforms.

The draw proceedings will take place as follows:

· TotalEnergies CAF Confederation Cup:14h00 Cairo time (11h00GMT)

· TotalEnergies CAF Champions League:15h00 Cairo time (12h00GMT)

The draws will determine the Group Stage opponents for Africa's leading clubs as they continue their pursuit of continental glory.

Four former African internationals will serve as Draw Assistants for the two competitions.

Burkina Faso's Alain Traoré and Sudan's Mudather Eltaieb will assist with the TotalEnergies CAF Confederation Cup Draw, while Tunisia's Oussama Darragi and Cameroon's Achille Emana will assist with the TotalEnergies CAF Champions League Draw.

Fans can follow the draw proceedings and reactions across CAF's digital platforms using #TotalEnergiesCAFCL and #TotalEnergiesCAFCC.

WATCH LIVE: CAF TV - Official YouTube Channel

For more information on the TotalEnergies CAF Champions League and TotalEnergies CAF Confederation Cup, visit www.cafonline.com.

#86. Africa: CAF to Host Clubs Workshop in Cairo to Prepare Qualified Teams for 2026/27 Interclub Group Stage

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

· CAF to bring together representatives of clubs in the Group Stage of the CAF Interclub Competitions

· Two-day workshop to focus on competition regulations, refereeing, VAR, commercial obligations and media operationsThe Confédération Africaine de Football ("CAF") will hold a two-day Clubs Workshop this week to prepare qualified clubs for the start of the Group Stages of the TotalEnergies CAF Champions League and TotalEnergies CAF Confederation Cup 2026/27.

The workshop, which is scheduled for Thursday, 29 October and Friday, 30 October 2026 will take place in Cairo, Egypt, bringing together club representatives to address key technical, administrative and operational requirements ahead of the continental competitions.

It will take place one day after the CAF Interclub Group Stage Draw on Wednesday, 28 October 2026, at the Egyptian Football Association Headquarters in Cairo, where the 16 qualified clubs in each competition will discover their opponents.

Follow us on WhatsApp | LinkedIn for the latest headlines

The workshop will cover competition regulations, match organisation, refereeing, Video Assistant Referee (VAR) procedures, anti-doping requirements and club responsibilities.

Participating clubs will also receive guidance on commercial regulations, television production, digital activities and media operations, as well as stadium safety and security, disciplinary matters and professional football.

The sessions will provide an opportunity for club officials to engage directly with CAF's relevant departments, clarify competition procedures and strengthen coordination ahead of the Group Stages.

The initiative forms part of CAF's continued efforts under the leadership of President Dr Patrice Motsepe to improve professionalism, operational standards and the financial sustainability of African club football.

CAF has increased its financial investment in the two competitions, with the TotalEnergies CAF Champions League winners set to receive a record USD 6 million, while the TotalEnergies CAF Confederation Cup champions will earn USD 4 million.

CAF will also provide USD 100,000 to clubs eliminated in the Preliminary Rounds to assist with travel and other logistical expenses, further supporting clubs participating in continental football.

The increased financial support forms part of CAF's broader strategy to improve the competitiveness of its Interclub competitions, strengthen the financial sustainability of African clubs and expand opportunities for teams across the continent.

#173. Which footballers have scored in the same minute as their surname? | The Knowledge

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Plus: the longest home droughts, pre-Christmas goal centuries and the first official club websites

Mail us with all of your questions and answers

“Justin Seven-Seven came on in the 75th minute and then scored in the 77th minute for West Brom Under-21s against Sutton United in the National League Cup. You have probably already guessed my question, but has anyone else ever scored in the same minute as their name?” wonders Peter Skilton.

Readers of a certain age may recall Didier Six, the mercurial French winger who had a brief spell at Aston Villa in 1984-85. A number of you mentioned some or all of the following sixth-minute goals, which includes two in consecutive seasons against Bordeaux:

Valenciennes 2-1 Nancy (agg: 2-3) Coupe de France quarter-final, 1975-76

Valenciennes 3-2 Bordeaux Ligue 1, 1976-77

VfB Stuttgart 5-1 Karlsruher Bundesliga, 1981-82

We’d imagine Alphonse Six, who won nine caps for Belgium and scored goals galore at club level before he died at 24 years old during the first world war, managed at least one in the sixth minute, but goal times for club football in the 1910s are not easy to come by. Pete Tomlin also offers “Austrian striker Martin Six, who also scored a goal in the sixth minute of a match. He was playing for SV Allerheiligen against DSV Leoben in the Regional League Central on the 1 April 2011.”

“Scottish lower league mainstay Armand Oné scored for Stranraer in the first minute of a League Two game against East Stirlingshire in 2010-11,” writes Richard Wilson, and yes we do believe it. “At the time of writing, son Ryan – at Sheffield United – hasn’t managed the feat, but he has plenty of time.”

This isn’t just an English-language question, as Filippo Varanini points out. “Some Italian football nerd (not me, unfortunately) dug up a Serie C game from 1998, when Raffaele Quaranta (whose surname means 40) scored for Casarano in the 40th minute.” Pete checks back in to mention that “Swiss midfielder Claude Sixt does not strictly have the same name as the number, but he did score a 60th-minute goal in the Swiss League for Sion v Urania Genève on 28 May 1966. Also, Marculino Ninte scored a 90th-minute goal for CD Mafra v SU 1° Dezembro in the third tier of Portuguese football on 5 October 2025.”

We will leave the final, not entirely serious, word to Mark Johnson. “On 2 August 2003, in a match between Banik Ostrava against Slovan Liberec, Marek Heinz (57 varieties) scored in the 57th minute.”

“Burnley have gone a calendar year without a home league win. What is the longest home drought, in the league or in all competitions?” asks our own Niall McVeigh.

Burnley beat Millwall in the FA Cup in January, but it’s 376 days since their last league win at Turf Moor, a 2-0 victory over Leeds on 18 October 2025. Still, could be worse …

“In the NWSL, Sky Blue FC (now Gotham FC) went without a home win between 26 August 2017 and 8 September 2018, a stretch of 378 days,” writes Dave Mellinger. “The victory came over Orlando Pride in their last game of the 2018 season and was their only win, home or away. Had they failed to win, the streak would have rolled on to the start of the next season in April 2019. Unfortunately for us stats nerds, they shocked the world and won that final match.”

Reggiana made no such mistake in Serie A 30 years ago. “Apologies for going all Football Italia on you again,” writes Daz Pearce, labouring under the misapprehension that stimulating the Proustian rush of Football Italia is an act worthy of apology, “but I was quite amused by Reggiana’s magnificent winless home at run in 1996-97, especially as they managed to nick two victories on the road.” Reggiana won their last home game of 1995-96 and the first of 1997-98, both in Serie B. But they did not win a single match at Stadio Giglio in 1996-97, not even in the cups. Their winlessness lasted from 23 May 1996 to 30 August 1997 – a whopping 465 days.

“By my reckoning, Ross County have already scored 81 goals this season: 38 in 10 league games (including beating poor Queen of the South 8-0 and 7-1), 30 in six Challenge Cup group games, 13 in six League Cup games. They may well have scored a century of goals by the end of October! Surely that cannot be bettered. What is the earliest a team has hit a century of goals in a season?” asks Stephen James.

Before we get to a pre-Christmas century from the distant past, we need a modern reference point. Last season, Vincent Kompany’s Bayern Munich reached 100 goals during a 5-1 win at Leipzig on 17 January. This was the breakdown:

Franz Beckenbauer Supercup 2 goals in 1 game

But the Turnbull Tornadoes – as the Hibernian side of the early-1970s, managed by Eddie Turnbull, were known – can beat that by a month. “In 1972-73, Hibernian reached their century of goals on 16 December after beating Ayr United 8-1,” notes Mark Shipway. “They had 41 league goals by then, in 14 games, and had also scored 12 in the Drybrough Cup (three games), 32 in the League Cup (12 games) and 15 in the European Cup Winners Cup (four games).”

Hibs played more games than Bayern, 33 to 28, but that wasn’t the question so pipe down. Their answer to Harry Kane, Michael Olise and Luis Díaz was Alan Gordon, Jimmy O’Rourke and Arthur Duncan, who scored 98 goals between them across the whole season.

That included six of the goals in a legendary 7-0 win at Hearts on 1 January. Weirdly, Hibs’ season ended in a binary fug. In the last eight games, starting with a Cup Winners’ Cup quarter-final defeat to Hajduk Split, the team scored only twice.

“Who were the first football club to have an official website?” wondered Chris White in 2016. “And how about an unofficial website? I noticed that TWTD, Ipswich Town’s unofficial fan site, has been going since 1995, so surely must be one of the first. Also, who were the first clubs to start using social media platforms?”

“I can help,” yelled Martyn Amos, thrusting his arm in the air. “Ipswich Town were, indeed, the first club to have a website (unofficial or otherwise). In fact, I used to help maintain it. It was set up in 1990 by Phil Clarke, who worked at BT in Martlesham (near Ipswich) when the web was still very much experimental. He then passed it on (1993-1995) to Paul Felton, who was a student at UEA in Norwich (boo, hiss).

“After Paul graduated, he passed it on to me as I was, at the time, a PhD student at Warwick (1995-96). Paul then took it back in 1996 and ran it until 2001, when the club finally woke up to the potential of the web, and got a local company called AWS to run it … There used to be some discussion with Sheffield United fans about our claim to primacy, but I believe that it was decided in our favour.”

As for unofficial websites … “Very likely to be Reading’s Hob Nob Anyone which has been knocking around since November 1994,” reckons Peter Sorrill. “Although the distinction between official and unofficial was moot in the early 90s as most clubs weren’t that tech savvy. Here’s the Evening Post’s report on its 20th anniversary from 2014.” In it, the founder Graham Loader explains that “I did computing and then I found out about the internet and realised you could do email and all that stuff. I didn’t think it would get big but it got quite big quite quickly. People would contact me and say how exciting it was that they could read about Reading in places like Canada and Australia.”

But Alex Lopez-Ortiz thought he could go earlier. “The ‘What’s New, September 1993’ NCSA webpage lists: ‘Information on the Sheffield Wednesday Football Club at Nottingham University [website] is online.’ This is very early in the life of the web. NCSA mosaic, which was what popularised the web, was introduced in January of that year.”

“In their 3-0 win over Milan, Bournemouth scored two penalties in the first half taken by different players against two different goalkeepers. Has this ever happened before? And, excluding shootouts, what is the record for the number of penalties scored by one team in a game by different players?” wonders Matthew Wilkie.

“What is the soonest a manager has been sacked after signing a contract extension?” asks Dave Smythe.

“Who was the first manager to wear a tracksuit while watching his team play?” muses Gordon Smith. “I remember that Sir Alf Ramsey used to wear one for England games so did he wear a tracksuit when he managed Ipswich?”

“Newcastle played Hull at 3pm on Saturday 19 September and will play Fulham at 3pm on 7 November at 3pm. All the previous fixtures haven’t been the traditional time,” begins Mark Reilly. “None of our planned fixtures to 7 January against Manchester United are Saturday 3pm kick-offs. What is the fewest number of Saturday 3pm kick-offs in a Premier League season by a club?”

Mail us with all of your questions and answers

#246. La principal plataforma china de contenidos, televisión e internet retira de su parrilla el Racing-Real Madrid

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

'Migu' cuenta con más de 400 millones de suscriptores

Migu, la principal y gigantesca plataforma china de contenidos, televisión e internet, que cuenta con más de 400 millones de suscriptores, ha retirado de su parrilla el partido Racing-Real Madrid del próximo domingo, correspondiente a la undécima jornada de Liga.

Los acontecimientos se siguen sucediendo desde que se conociese que la CMG, propietaria de los derechos de LaLiga en China, anunciaba que dejaba de retransmitir únicamente los partidos en los que participe Mbappé, después de que el francés reconociera que se ríe con los 'memes' que lo comparan con Mao Zedong. A partir de ahí, las consecuencias de la polémica se han ido sucediendo. Varias empresas chinas, entre ellas el grupo de bebidas Dongpeng y las compañías lácteas Mengniu y Milkground, anunciaron el cese de su cooperación comercial con el futbolista del Real Madrid. Una polémica que ha coincidido con el pleno del partido comunista chino, reunión a puerta cerrada que se prolongará hasta el jueves, y hasta donde ha llegado el 'caso Mbappé', un altercado que ha provocado que la imagen del Real Madrid en China esté seriamente dañada.

Mbappé tuvo que salir al paso de la polémica generada por sus declaraciones sobre China que han provocado un descomunal malestar en el país asiático. "Durante una entrevista reciente, reaccioné a una imagen mía en la que aparecía representado. Mi comentario fue desafortunado y ahora comprendo que ha herido a muchas personas en China y entre la comunidad china de todo el mundo", explicaba el futbolista francés en un comunicado firmado este martes 27 de octubre en París. El delantero madridista cerraba su comunicado con una disculpa y un mensaje de respeto hacia el país asiático: "Pido disculpas a todas las personas a las que mis palabras hayan ofendido. No era en absoluto mi intención. Siento un profundo respeto por el pueblo chino y su historia, y estoy sinceramente arrepentido".

Con este mensaje, Mbappé trataba de zanjar una polémica que había obligado intervenir a LaLiga. La patronal y su presidente, Javier Tebas, lamentaron el malestar causado y aseguraron que trabajaban con sus socios chinos para proteger la relación y evitar perjuicios a los clubes. "Entendemos el daño ocasionado y, en nombre de LALIGA, quiero trasladar nuestras sinceras disculpas al pueblo chino", afirma Tebas en un mensaje publicado en su cuenta de X. El propio Javier Tebas dio otro paso más en su intención de enfriar la polémica creada por la repercusión que han tenido en China las declaraciones de Kylian Mbappé. El presidente de LaLiga, tras pedir disculpas al pueblo chino tanto con con un mensaje en su cuenta de X como con un comunicado en la web de LaLiga, publicó un mensaje posterior, acompañado de una foto, de la reunión mantenida con el embajador de China en España.

#147. Kylian Mbappé apologises after being dropped by Chinese brands for Mao Zedong comment

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Video appears to show Mao Zedong called a ‘dictator’

Dongpeng and Mengniu end deals with the player

Kylian Mbappé has apologised after the Real Madrid forward was dropped by major Chinese brands for apparently joking about Mao Zedong in a video that has gone viral. There were also signs of broader repercussions, with the state-run Chinese digital newspaper The Paper reporting that public television was suspending broadcasts of matches from La Liga.

The offending video shows Mbappé saying that the funniest internet meme he had seen involving him was of his face photoshopped on to that of “some guy – I think he’s a Chinese dictator”. This was an apparent reference to a meme of Mbappé superimposed on an image of Mao.

“My comment was ill-judged, and I now understand that it hurt many people in China and in Chinese communities around the world. I apologise to everyone my words have upset,” Mbappé said in a statement in French, Chinese and English.

Spain’s La Liga also apologised, saying it “deeply regrets the offence that Kylian Mbappé’s recent comments have caused among Chinese fans. These comments were made in a personal capacity and do not represent La Liga.” In China, jokes about Mao, the country’s founding leader who died in 1976 – and whose drastic policies are also blamed by historians for mass starvation and millions of deaths – remain taboo.

The beverage giant Dongpeng, which during this year’s World Cup ran adverts with AI-generated images of Mbappé consuming its sport drink, said it was terminating all cooperation with the France captain. “Our company firmly opposes and has a zero-tolerance policy towards any behaviour that offends national sentiments,” Dongpeng said in a statement.

Mengniu, China’s largest dairy producer, and cheese maker Milkground also said that they were cutting ties with Mbappé, who until now was hugely popular among Chinese fans with millions of social media followers. “Dongpeng Beverage ends cooperation with Mbappé” was the top-trending topic on Chinese social media site Weibo with 430m views.

One Weibo user said the “extremely serious wrong remarks … crossed the red line” and others urged Dongpeng to remove Mbappé’s image from their bottles. Dongqiudi, one of China’s biggest online football outlets, said Mbappé’s remarks were “offensive to China and the Chinese people”.

In an interview with the French sports newspaper L’Equipe last month, the 27-year-old Mbappé addressed a previous meme depicting him as a military dictator. “There’s a part of it that’s funny … but there is another part that’s a bit less funny because of the damage such people have caused throughout history,” he said.

China’s foreign ministry said it was aware of the reports regarding Mbappé, who on Monday finished runner-up to Harry Kane in the Ballon d’Or award for the world’s best player. “Words and actions that hurt the feelings of the Chinese people will naturally be met with unanimous rejection from all sectors of Chinese society,” spokesperson Guo Jiakun told a regular briefing.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

The Paper cited sources at the state broadcaster saying that CCTV has suspended all broadcasts of La Liga matches, and removed all advertisements involving Mbappé. On the CCTV website, La Liga matches this weekend were still on its online viewing schedule.

Global sports stars who have commented on sensitive topics such as Taiwan, Tibet and Hong Kong have previously caused controversy in China and been dumped by sponsors there. The former NBA player Dwight Howard referred to Taiwan as a country in 2023 as he appeared in a video with the island’s then vice-president and current president, Lai Ching-te, sparking fury on Chinese social media. Beijing claims Taiwan is part of its territory and has threatened to use force to bring it under its control.

The Houston Rockets general manager, Daryl Morey, posted a tweet in 2019 supporting Hong Kong protests, leading to China suspending sponsorship deals with the NBA and pulling games off Chinese television for a year.

Fans in China made their feelings clear. “I used to really enjoy watching Mbappé play on the pitch,” Zhang Jingqi, 30, who works at a mall in Beijing, said. “But ever since this news broke yesterday I have lost that admiration, sorry to say.”

#393. El Sardinero se vuelca con el regreso del Real Madrid: entradas agotadas en menos de dos horas

📅 Oct 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

El Racing vendió las cerca de 4.000 localidades en apenas 116 minutos

La visita del Real Madrid a Santander ha desatado una auténtica locura. El Racing agotó este lunes en menos de dos horas todas las entradas que había puesto a la venta para el partido que enfrentará a ambos equipos el próximo domingo en los Campos de Sport de El Sardinero, confirmando la enorme expectación que ha generado el regreso del conjunto blanco a la capital cántabra quince años después.

A las 10.00 horas se abría el proceso de venta digital para abonados y simpatizantes racinguistas. Apenas 116 minutos más tarde, a las 11.56, ya no quedaba ni una sola localidad disponible de las cerca de 4.000 que el club había puesto en circulación. Una respuesta masiva que refleja la dimensión de un encuentro marcado en rojo desde que se conoció el calendario.

Los aficionados se lanzaron desde primera hora a la plataforma online del Racing para asegurarse una plaza en un choque que promete un ambiente de gala. Ni siquiera unos precios que partían de los 50 euros para adultos frenaron la demanda. Las entradas infantiles y júnior, por su parte, estaban disponibles desde 24 y 32 euros, respectivamente.

Con el lleno prácticamente garantizado, el club cántabro activará este lunes desde las 13.00 horas la opción de cesión de asiento a través del Portal del Abonado, permitiendo que los socios que no puedan acudir al encuentro pongan sus localidades a disposición de otros aficionados.

La presencia del Real Madrid vuelve a convertir El Sardinero en el gran escenario del fútbol español por un día. La última visita oficial del conjunto madridista a Santander se remonta a hace 15 años, un factor que ha multiplicado el interés por un partido que reunirá a miles de aficionados deseosos de ver de cerca al equipo blanco.

#307. Paris Saint-Germain sparkle on the pitch while the city of light burns around them | Barney Ronay

📅 Oct 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

PSG, pet project of the state of Qatar, expresses the dualism of the city, a place of deprivation where the centre is perfect

Those who sought to bring down the Republic will have to answer for it.

Tell you who would have loved this? Who would have been utterly engrossed scrolling the hashtag #Paris on Tuesday night as two things happened simultaneously, the harrowing of the Barcelona defence at the Parc des Princes, and the harrowing of Paris itself via tales of city-centre thuggery and end-of-days political doom‑mongering.

The big man, Guy Debord himself. The Society of the Spectacle, you say? Actually, I think we’re past that now. Hold the society bit. We’ll just take the spectacle.

The great Parisian Situationist philosopher wasn’t really one for managed fun. But it was impossible not to be bowled over by the extraordinary pre-match show for Paris Saint‑Germain versus Barcelona, Champions League Matchday Three.

The wild pyrotechnics, the plumes of smoke, the full-wrap light show, the lime-green robo‑text spelling out every player’s name on the pitch. Although not the Barcelona names, sadly, for anyone with an unrealised desire to see the words “ANTHONY GORDON” projected in vast letters for the delight of a shrieking crowd.

Admittedly, the Halloween‑themed tifo banner showing a masked Paris Saint-Germain serial killer-type literally butchering the Barcelona badge to death with his bloodied chainsaw was perhaps a bit much. As were the Barça Puta signs, which are now reportedly the subject of a complaint to Uefa.

But it was a wild and all‑consuming spectacle, annihilating every other impulse in your head to a green thought in a green shade. And in the process capturing the profound sense of cognitive dissonance in the city.

Just walking around the streets of central Paris earlier in the day generated its own mild whiplash. On the face of it this was still the same city of flâneurs and tourists and everyday bistro business, and not, repeat not, a civil war drama or some kind of emergent military caliphate.

But the sense of dread was also tangible. You still got the occasional crunch of glass underfoot. Here and there charred bikes and scooters had been politely piled to one side. Even close to the Parc, away from the main hotspots, shopkeepers could be seen emerging warily into their doorways at the sound of a police siren.

By Tuesday night, a total of 2,513 people had been taken into police custody after the events of the weekend. At least 420 rental bikes had been destroyed or maimed. Liberated high-value retail goods were already being sold on Vinted, a commendable show of entrepreneurial energy from the maligned younger generation.

Not that you’d suspect these two worlds had any kind of overlap, lost in the booming triumphalism around PSG’s 4–2 win, one of the most beautifully luminous football matches you’re ever likely to see. Or indeed reading the coverage of an event that seems to have been treated in France like its own clear, clean space, floating above the flames, all the chat about the brilliance of Monsieur Dynamite, Désiré Doué, the wonderful television audiences on Canal+ TV, the whole of European football “kneeling before the machine” (are we sure this isn’t to do with anarchic rejection of social control?). “Paris est insubmersible!” was the headline in Le Parisien. But is it?

Remember when football used to reflect the world? Or just provide clues, a commentary, some kind of metaphor? This is no longer the case. At a time when even the most thunderous of commentators is struggling to summarise all this in suitably trite and damning terms, it was hard to avoid the feeling Paris Saint-Germain, the pet power football project of the state of Qatar, expresses this dualism perfectly.

Sometimes you just have to let football tell you things. “People who want to cause trouble in the city centres next Saturday will be prevented,” Emmanuel Macron announced on Tuesday evening, speaking, as it happens, from Doha, where he met Sheikh Tamim bin Hamad al-Thani, ultimate owner of PSG.

Macron is said to be considering deploying the French military to keep the peace on the streets before the next planned protests, although even here he may have to ask Qatar’s permission if he wants to deploy any of the French troops currently deployed working its missile systems, or embedded in the Doha security services.

The key event Macron is tooling up for is this Saturday night, 24 October, the date the gilets jaunes social media feed has set for an even greater swell of disorder. And again this will be football adjacent, with PSG playing Lyon at home on Sunday, a top‑of‑the-table capital‑city clash played out against whatever wreckage is left, however many thousands of Dot bikes are savaged, whatever guerrilla skirmishes are still playing out in the Marais.

What does this really have to do with football and the Ici C’est Paris brand appropriated by a state outreach project? Well, what are these demonstrations anyway? There have been many theories put forward as to what exactly is happening here, what is the spark, the kindling, the fire behind this disorder. School protests were the original line. Others have now roped in some kind of nebulous omni-cause, Instagram-activism, perhaps even a barbarian takeover, the decline of the west, savagery at the gates, of the type Donald Trump pretends to see.

Do you need a single reason? Take a look around. Modern life is pretty alarming on a minute‑to‑minute basis. Disaffection, alienation, a sense of being transformed in real time into a passive economic consumer unit, a set of eyeballs, an avatar of consumption. French youth unemployment is close to 20%. Robots nobody wants are being lined up to take jobs that don’t exist. Why don’t these young people want to join a youth club? Why don’t they sign up for the Liberal Democrats? Why are they enjoying empowered disorder? Maybe because nothing is yours around here, not even the things thrust into your brain as digital distractions. So why not grab something when you can? Debord would have called this “a vicious circle of isolation”, lonely crowd, technologies based on isolation.

On the other hand, what is PSG in Paris, other than the perfect expression of this? It often seems to surprise people that celebrations of PSG victories end up with riots and burnt-out cars. But Paris is both a beautiful and deeply dysfunctional city, a place of make‑believe and deprivation, where the centre is a perfect, performative relic, the suburbs shunted out to the ring roads, a life lived inside a box, noses pressed up against the glass, peering through your binoculars at the city of someone else’s love.

Paris is the divide between overclass and churn expressed in a single city. It’s everything you might have had but never will. And PSG represent this like no other entity, a football club owned as an act of outreach by a staggeringly rich inherited monarchy, weaponised passion, borrowed culture, engineered spectacle, deeply cynical but also deeply seductive.

Why are the Qataris splurging hundreds of millions into this project? Because they just really like high-pressing positional football? Qatar is the indentured labour state that employs Colin Kaepernick to promote its outreach projects. It will hymn its connections to the footballers of the banlieue, which is true and real and valuable, but not because of anything this political project wants or needs other than public relations.

It is in its own way a perfect human lab experiment, synthesised beauty, collectivism, with a cold, cold hand at its heart. And this is why there weren’t two versions of Paris in evidence on Tuesday night, just the same one, the dark side and the shiny side, an engine of the same divisions, the same kind of violence, right down to the sight of kids in PSG training gear dodging the police or taunting the barricades at the Place de la République.

And now we have Sunday night to come, more Ici C’est Paris, more PASSION. What kind of background will it be played out against? Does it matter under the lights? Will anyone notice?

Le Monde has described these events as a very basic test of the republic, its ability to bend, to remain essentially itself, the state’s willingness to pit itself against its own people. Football, as ever, holds these answers already. PSG’s Qatari owners have spent the past 15 years flaunting this city like a global luxury brand, extending its reach, creating its own ideal of conspicuous consumption, spectacle, triumph, son et lumière, bread and roses. As ever, if you want to know how and why, football has the answers.

#389. The part-timers who won three Wembley finals in a row

📅 Sep 30, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The part-timers who won three Wembley finals in a row

Question: Which two men's club sides have won three successive competition finals at the new Wembley?

You probably knew Pep Guardiola's Manchester City won three League Cups on the bounce between 2018 and 2020 before adding a fourth in 2021 â but another side did the silverware hat-trick first.

Whitley Bay, who play in the ninth-tier Northern Football League, won three successive FA Vase finals between 2009 and 2011 â an unprecedented achievement in non-league football.

The FA Vase, a national competition for amateur teams, was established in 1974; and in more than half a century The Seahorses are the only ones to win it four times â including those three on the trot.

And they did so under the eye of an unassuming IT manager from Sunderland, who used "quiet moments in the day" to plan historic cup success.

Ian Chandler, who had three spells at Whitley Bay as a player, became manager in 2004 and spent 10 years in charge.

It was no mean feat, as any non-league manager will tell you. Instead of focusing on work meetings, Chandler says he spent more time sorting hotel bookings or discussing scouting reports.

His team were inspired by missing out on Wembley in 2007-08, when Whitley Bay reached the FA Vase semi-finals. They lost 4-0 to Lowestoft Town in the first leg, and despite going 3-0 up inside 20 minutes of the return fixture at Bay's Hillheads Park stadium they were unable to overturn the deficit.

After that elimination, Chandler developed a superstition of never again giving pre-match interviews - as the last time he did so was before the 4-0 defeat.

Whether because of his renewed focus, or because of a desire among players to make amends, it steeled the group to make history.

"It's the hardest thing to keep a team together - as soon as players get noticed, they get picked up at higher level," Chandler tells BBC Sport.

"But after we got to the Vase semis, players wanted to stay and prove what they could do.

Ian Chandler played for Barnsley, Stockport and Aldershot before a successful non-league career

Despite being a lifelong Sunderland fan, Chandler admits basing his team on Kevin Keegan's Newcastle - with entertainment even placed above winning.

"There was a standing joke in the team, two or three at the back? One time we chased a game with two at the back," he says.

"The Premier League can be so boring; fans want to be entertained, that's what we promised."

Chandler was already a Whitley Bay hero when he was appointed manager by chairman Paul McIlduff, having scored the winner in the 2002 FA Vase final against Tiptree United at Villa Park.

After a slow start as the then-35-year-old with no prior managerial experience found his bearings, Chandler built a talented team which became the envy of non-league football nationwide.

"We played good football. We had pace on the wing, fantastic midfielders, and strikers who were deadly," says McIlduff, who has been Whitley Bay chairman since 2001.

"When we brought them in, it was about fitting the psychology of the dressing room. No-one thought they were better than anyone else, they played for each other."

A growing influence in the side was the 'Whitley Bay Maradona'.

Paul Robinson came through Newcastle United's academy before a career spent across various non-league clubs in the north-east â including Whitley Bay from 2007 to 2013.

Like several other players and staff, he worked with Chandler both in his day job at software company Sage and at Hillheads Park.

"He was my manager at work and football, I was sick of him," he tells BBC Sport. "But it helped get time off for matches."

And it meant he also had an understanding boss if his mind drifted at work.

"Zoning out happened regularly, picturing scoring and winning, what the opposition would be like - hoping they were rubbish so we could have an easy stroll," he says.

Perhaps Robinson's greatest Whitley Bay moment came in the 2010 FA Vase semi-final second leg, when his last-minute diving header in the driving rain secured a 6-5 aggregate victory over Barwell, sparking a pitch invasion.

"Everyone goes to the finals, you were there, you could enjoy it," says Robinson, who is now academy head at South Shields.

"But the rounds before were the bigger games. At home, we would get 2,000 or 3,000 at every game. You felt like a real player. You associate those memories with 90s FA Cup you never think it will happen in non-league."

Chandler played in a 2-0 win over Preston in round two as they reached the FA Cup third round for the first and only time in the 1989-90 season, and they also progressed to round two the following campaign.

Those seasons of success - made The Bay a feared side for non-league clubs across England, and meant they were ready for the big games in a way most amateur sides are not.

"One quarter-final against Biggleswade [in 2009], they turned up in suits and ties," says Robinson. "We thought, this is their day out, their cup final. We won 5-2.

"By the end, teams were turning up afraid of us. We had it won beforehand."

Paul Robinson scored the fifth goal in Whitley Bay's 6-1 win over Wroxham in the 2010 FA Vase final

Off the pitch, the team's success had an influence on the whole of Whitley Bay. A working-class town near Newcastle, which had its heyday as a Victorian-era holiday destination, sporting success brought local pride to the north-east coast.

And like any lower league club, that success could only be supported by dedicated volunteers, who went into overdrive before the Vase finals.

They would go door to door across shops in Tynemouth, North Shields and Whitley Bay to raise support. A van selling tickets was parked outside Woolworths on the high street.

"There was such excitement around the whole of Whitley Bay - every shop had things in the windows, the blue and white stripes," says one of the volunteers, Jenn Robinson.

She got involved because her late husband Mike was a club board member and if she wanted to see him she "had to go around there".

"Everyone wanted to come and watch, we got so many local sponsors," adds Jenn.

"I still do buffets and things for players and long-standing fans. We have a game tonight, and I'm making the sandwiches now."

Newcastle United helped out with selling tickets for the finals, while North Tyneside Council provided security services as thousands flocked to Hillheads Park for big cup games.

But even the best laid plans can go awry.

"We organised a train down to London, the guarantee was that it would go straight to Wembley," says McIlduff. "The first time it worked, second time they were taken to Kings Cross - we had to organise taxis up to Wembley."

By hook or by crook, The Seahorses army made it three times to the national stadium. Some even got royal treatment.

"It was absolutely surreal, and a delight," Jenn says. "The first Wembley final we played at, it had just opened. My husband being on the board, we sat in the royal box, which was fabulous.

"One of the security guys showing me to our seats said, 'Miss Robinson, this is Prince William's seat'. He hadn't even sat in it at that point."

Thousands of fans travelled from Whitley Bay to London across the three finals

Chandler, whose son Benjamin was a mascot at all three finals, said it was an overwhelming experience at times for the players too.

"The first year was crazy, everything was brand new," he says. "The first year the bus driver scraped the side up of the bus driving into the ground.

"The second year we tried to take everything in, all the lads had their mobiles. Then the third year, it was just a case of can we do it, win three in a row."

And three times, Whitley Bay rose to the occasion.

After beating Glossop North End 2-0 in 2009 in front of 12,200 supporters, Whitley Bay returned a year later to thrash Wroxham 6-1.

The opening goal was scored by prolific striker Paul Chow after only 21 seconds. At the time this was the quickest goal in a Wembley final and was only surpassed 13 years later when Ilkay Gundogan scored for Manchester City inside 13 seconds in the FA Cup final.

The hat-trick was completed in 2011 with a frantic 3-2 win over Coalville Town, who equalised twice before Chow was again the hero with an 86th-minute winner.

In total, nearly 30,000 fans were in the stands across the three finals.

After each final, Whitley Bay celebrated in The Green Man pub just 15 minutes' walk from the famous Wembley arch.

"In the first year we just went back to the bar and it was an anti-climax," Robinson says. "The second and third years the club got their act together and we rented a room; families, friends and fans were invited, they were brilliant nights.

"In 2010, we actually spent 10 days together - the week of the final we spent as a squad preparing, then we went to Marbella for five nights. Best 10 days of my life!"

The 2010 trophy lift was perhaps the most emotional though, as the players were joined by former team-mate Mark Taylor. He had played in the previous year's final, before being diagnosed with motor neurone disease, forcing an early retirement.

"Mark used to come on the away trips after he got diagnosed, he just wanted to be Mark - we had a good laugh everywhere we went," says Chandler. "He's still knocking about, abusing me on Facebook. He was the core of our second and third year."

Whitley Bay lift the trophy in 2010, joined by former team-mate Mark Taylor (left)

Success ensured the lives of Chandler and his team were never quite the same again. Reporters turned up to Sage to speak with the manager and his players; even Chandler's mum was interviewed by BBC Radio Newcastle legend Paul Dixon.

Chandler - and his wife - even got up close and personal with managerial icons.

"I was invited to the annual sports awards in Manchester. There were people there like Sven Goran Eriksson and Sir Alex Ferguson [and] numerous others," he says.

"I had my wife with me and Sven came over gave her a peck on the cheek - she wasn't aware of who it was until I told her later that evening."

Robinson admitted that going back to work at an IT company after Wembley glory was a "massive comedown".

"One year I got a standing ovation when I walked into the office, but after that it was just back to normal," he says.

"People wind you up about it - 'are you talking about Wembley again? Give it a rest Robbo!"

Like all good things, Whitley Bay's golden era came to an end. Chandler departed in 2014 after a dip in form; the club have not won a major honour since the 2011 FA Vase and remain in the ninth tier.

But the memories in the football-mad north-east remain.

"Me and my wife went out on a Sunday at a pub in Durham just a few weeks back, and this guy came up out the blue and asked, 'Are you Ian Chandler?'" he laughs.

"Then he started talking about that team; in the north-east, everyone follows football. It was so lovely."

#374. 'I persuaded my club to give me a job as a content creator'

📅 Sep 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Making videos with fans and players at his local football club is all in a day's work for Jacob.

He's a content creator for Bolton Wanderers FC, coming up with new ideas for social media, filming videos and posting them onto official pages. The aim is to encourage people to follow the club.

If someone would've said to me I'd be doing this as a job when I was 12, I wouldn't have believed them.

My name's Jacob. I'm a content creator, and I work with Bolton Wanderers.

My role here at Bolton is coming up with new ideas for social media, to try new concepts, filming stuff, and then posting them onto Bolton's pages and trying to help people follow the club. In my opinion, I think it's one of the funnest jobs in football, and it's brilliant.

Right. Welcome to Bolton, Larra. I hope I said that right. I'm going to teach you some Bolton twang to help you settle in.

My passion for football when I was growing up started at a very young age. My dad used to take me to the football matches, and I got my very first season ticket when I was five years old in the 2010/11 season, when Bolton was in the Premiership. Being something I loved as a kid, to turn it into my job is amazing.

I work here at least two days a week, obviously filming content, producing content,and then on match day, I'll be filming mostly with fans, and I love it.

As a town? Well, for us, it's lovely. Yeah.

Mondays, meetings. We have the marketing meeting, so that gives you an idea of what the week holds, what I'm going to do on a match day with the fans. And then in the week, I either film in with players, retail, commercial,whatever department needs help producing videos.

Some of the skills I use, I think one of them's got to be communication. You've got to be able to talk to people, talk to fans, and if you're watching this and you're a bit shy, or you think you can't communicate with people as well, I was like that inhigh school, so you learn it as you get older. But working in football, you've got to be adaptable because one minute you're doing a new signing, next minute you're working with the players, next minute it's a match day, next minute the season's over.

I'd say another one is being creative. That's probably the most important one because without being creative, you won't be able to put a video together.

So when I was 16 in high school, that's where my journey started. I did videos, which got me a number of followers. I always struggled in the classroom side in high school, but creating content and creating ideas and being creative, I was always really, really good at in high school, whether that would be doing something drama-related or English, writing, creating stories. I always knew I had a passion for creative pieces of work and being able to now do it as a job, it suits me down to the ground.

I didn't go to college. I didn't go to uni. I left high school with no GCSEs, but I've always had a real passion for football, so that's how I got in contact with Bolton Wanderers. I started doing some videos here at Bolton with the fans and that, and then it turned into going to the training ground and filming with the players, working with the commercial team, retail team, all the different departmentsaround the football club to eventually getting a paid role in football.

I think don't wait for an opportunity to come to you. Get out there and try. If you're good at it, it does then sometimes lead into getting a role there. But if it doesn't, then you've also created a portfolio working with different people, different businesses, different companies, and it's really good for your CV and for future opportunities with different companies.

The highlight of my career so far, probably looking back, being resilient enough to create my own career path in doing something I love.

When you score this season, you might be chuffed.

If you're passionate about football, you're a bit creative, and you're good in front of the camera, content creation infootball might be a job for you.

It isn't just about making videos - content creators need to understanding their audience, respond to analytics and adapt material for different platforms

Jacob works as a content creator for Bolton Wanderers and his career progression has followed this path:

Voluntary work at Bolton --> Professional content creator

While it might look simple, he's had to be resilient - and one of the biggest obstacles he faced was convincing Bolton to create his role. "Don't wait for an opportunity to come to you," he says. "If you're good at it, it does then sometimes lead into getting a role. If it doesn't, you've created a portfolio and it's really good for your CV and future opportunities."

#368. 'My job combines my passion for gaming and football'

📅 Sep 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ever wondered who makes sure your footy video games are great? This guy!

CJ works as an associate quality assurance manager on the video game Football Manager. His team test the game to make sure everything works, and collaborate with the developers to create the most realistic simulation of football.

Working on one of the world's biggest football video games is the perfect combination of my two biggest passions, gaming and football.

I'm CJ Ramson. I work on the creation of football video games. I work on Football Manager as an associate QA manager. QA stands for quality assurance.

We're the ones that test the game. We make sure everything works, and we work closely with the developers to try and make the most realistic simulation of football.

Growing up, I loved football, whether that was playing football or playing football video games with my friends. I'd always be doing one or the other. With the video games, the thing that really drew me to them was I've always been into the tactical side of the game, either being able to control the players or create your own tactics.

So a typical day for us, we'll get a new build in from the developers. We'll test it as a QA team, and we'll look for either bugsor ways that we can improve the game. That can be anything from the runs that players make, to decisions they make on the ball, what the team instructions and tactics do. So there's a lot of areas for us to cover. Our final goal is to make it as realistic to real football as possible.

So even though my background was in coaching, there was a lot of transferable skills that I learned and developed through my time coaching that I managed to transfer into this. So whether that's being really analytical and having a good eye for detail and knowingwhen to pick things out and what to look for, or it could be things like timekeeping and making sure things run perfectly on time, and working with other people as well. So social skills where you have to work within a team or even lead a team.

The animation he makes, and the ball physics as well, where the ball goes off after the save."

So as much as I loved playing football, I realised quite early on I probably wasn't going to be a professionalfootballer. But I wanted to work in football and find. So at college, I studied sports science, and that's where I found my love of coaching. I did my FA Level 1 and then went on to do my FA Level 2. After I left college, I got a job at Arsenal, which was unpaid, working with them, learning how to develop my coaching skills. Which then led to me having a full-time paid job at Fulham Football Club, working with the youth teams, working with the academy teams. And then that eventually led to me going to an event. I saw an opportunity where you could apply for a job in Football Manager. It was something that naturally I was interested in, had a conversation with them, and realised it was definitely something that I would love to do.

So I went to the networking event at Kick It Out, and that was an opportunity for me to network with other football coaches, other people within the gaming industries, and have a really good insight to different jobs in football gaming. Without doing that, I wouldn't have the opportunity to meet the people at Football Manager and see a new job in gaming, which I wouldn't have beenaware of.

One of the highlights of my career so far is working on the in-and-out-of-possession system on FM 26. This is something we see in real football all the time, so being part of the team that brought that to life and brought it into the game was super cool.

Working within the football industry is a dream come true. Being able to work on something that I'm passionate about, that is played and enjoyed by millions of people, is an amazing feeling for me and an amazing opportunity that I have. And yeah, I love it.

To find out what other jobs you could have in football, explore the BBC Bitesize Follow Your Passion collection.

There are lots of different ways to progress in the gaming industry.

CJ works in the QA (Quality Assurance) team, testing games. In this team, his career progression has followed this path:

His team is just one of the options, though. "There are lots of other roles within the industry too, including production, game design, social media and development, where developers help to code the game," he explains. "Depending on your skills and interests, there are different pathways you can take that you might enjoy and be suited to."

There are different roles in video game testing. CJ notes that: "Salaries can vary depending on your role and the company you work for. They can range from around Â£20,000 to Â£50,000 and upwards." The higher salaries in this range could apply when you've gained more experienced and progressed in your career.

CJ is now a quality assurance manager, but he started out as a quality assurance tester. Find out more about the role of QA tester below. This role can have various other job titles, such as games tester, computer games tester, and video games tester.

You could get into this role via a university course, a college course (such as a Level 3 Diploma in Creative Media, an A-level in Computing, or a T-level in Digital Software Development (England only)), an apprenticeship, or applying directly if you have relevant experience.

This information is a guide and is constantly changing. Please check the National Careers Service website for the latest information and all the qualifications needed and the GOV.UK website for more on T-levels.

For careers advice in all parts of the UK visit: Get careers information and advice (England), nidirect (Northern Ireland), My World of Work (Scotland) and Careers Wales (Wales).

Bitesize Careers, in partnership with The Open University, has launched the Follow Your Passion campaign, exploring how you can draw on your passions and interests to find your future jobs, with the focus on football.

From content creation to computer game design, nutrition to number-crunching, check out the Bitesize Careers Follow Your Passion collection for loads of inspiring careers content.

Find more careers in football on the Bitesize Careers Follow Your Passion page.

#36. Preso a zero, ora vale 70 milioni: i segreti di Thuram, l'oro dell'Inter

📅 Dec 08, 2023  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Da zero a 70 in tre mesi e mezzo: Marcus viaggia veloce che è una bellezza. L’Inter ha trovato l’oro e adesso lo indossa, come fosse ogni partita la Prima della Scala, bello ed elegante come pochi altri. E prezioso, certo. Perché Thuram oggi vale già 70 milioni di euro. Quando in estate, al momento di firmare il contratto con il club nerazzurro, l’attaccante chiese di inserire una clausola da 95 milioni, la cifra pareva oggettivamente inattaccabile, se non agli occhi del più ottimista degli ottimisti. E invece adesso non siamo poi così distanti, da quel che in linea teorica oggi potrebbe chiedere l’Inter sul mercato per il suo gioiello.

Gioiello in oro, chiaro. Firmato Piero Ausilio, il d.s. che lo ha inseguito e corteggiato per due anni, prima di poterlo...indossare. Firmato pure Beppe Marotta, che a fine giugno - nei giorni in cui il Milan pareva aver preso la pole position - diede l’ok per l’accelerata con l’ultimo rialzo sull’ingaggio. Voltandosi indietro, a Marotta tornerà in mente il suo colpo a parametro zero per eccellenza, quel Paul Pogba arrivato da Manchester svincolato alla Juve e rivenduto (allo stesso United) per 115 milioni. Thuram nella scia del connazionale, allora. Perché proprio a Pogba può essere accostato, l’attaccante. Oppure allo stesso Onana. Ovvero calciatori non arrivati a scadenza perché avanti con la carta d’identità. Ma, più semplicemente, protagonisti corteggiati e strappati alla concorrenza con un progetto tecnico di alto livello, oltre chiaramente grazie all’offerta di un ingaggio top. Thuram è il paradigma perfetto di quel che si propone di fare l’Inter: valorizzazione di un asset sotto ogni punto di vista, economico e puramente sportivo.

Finora Marcus si è completamente immerso nell’interismo, al punto da citare nelle esultanze i grandi nerazzurri di ieri, ma pure i compagni di oggi. Ad esempio, si è messo le mani alle orecchie come il Mauro Icardi dei bei tempi a San Siro e a Napoli ha fatto roteare la maglia come Adriano. Ha pure ripetuto assieme a Dimarco il classico festeggiamento dell’esterno, ma quando ha imbracciato il fucile in stile Lukaku, Lautaro gli ha fatto no con la manina: forse è stato l’unico momento in cui la ThuLa non si è capita. Gioia ed esultanze a parte, l’impatto del francese è stato devastante sotto il profilo strettamente tecnico: tutto figlio di un’attenzione al lavoro e una cura del particolare che in casa nerazzurra ha colpito chiunque, a tutti i livelli. Thuram migliora chi gioca vicino a lui. Un paio di numeri, a supporto. Il primo è il 6: sei come gli assist di Marcus, nessuno in Serie A ha spinto un compagno al gol come lui. Il secondo numero è il 15: 15 come le reti stagionali di Lautaro, che mai in carriera era partito così bene. E certo, molto dipende dall’argentino in persona, ma il fatto di giocare vicino a Thuram è una leva non secondaria. La ThuLa è la coppia del campionato. E Thuram l’oro diventato luccicante nelle mani di Inzaghi. Il francese ha imparato velocemente un modulo con il quale praticamente non aveva mai giocato. Ancor di più: ha memorizzato i movimenti di una squadra già collaudata.

Guarda tutte le partite di Serie A Enilive in esclusiva nei locali Sky! Trova il locale più vicino a te.

Thuram è due attaccanti in uno. È prima e seconda punta insieme, è appoggio a Lautaro e ricerca della profondità allo stesso tempo. Ma, in fondo, deve ancora scoprire tutte le sue qualità. Forse è per questo che ama confrontarsi dopo ogni partita con papà Lilian, per capire dove ha fatto bene e dove invece deve migliorare. Passaggio che fa anche con lo staff di Inzaghi. Thuram - raccontano ad Appiano - è un divoratore di video che gli mettono a disposizione gli uomini della match analysis del club: così studia i difensori che affronterà nel match successivo, così sa valutare quanto fatto nella gara precedente. È in questo modo che si arriva in fretta a conquistare la Serie A. È così facendo che la valutazione schizza rapidamente a quota 70 milioni. È così, pure, che ci si prende una rivincita verso quei grandi club che non hanno puntato su di lui con convinzione: ogni riferimento a Bayern e Psg non è puramente casuale. Buon per l’Inter, che se lo gode tutto.

Marcus Thuram non ha abbracciato la sua nuova squadra come fanno tanti colleghi, distrattamente e senza particolare sentimento. Il francese si è immerso nel nerazzurro mani e piedi, ha fatto un bagno di interismo nel profondo: sarà anche per questo che i tifosi sono già pazzi di lui. Tutto studiato con i filmati di YouTube e realizzato alla prima occasione in partita.

#51. Inter, sotto Natale l'Udinese è indigesta: Inzaghi suona l'allarme

📅 Dec 08, 2023  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bijol esulta dopo il gol del 2-1 del settembre 2022. Getty

Simone Inzaghi sa che la partita contro l'Udinese per l'Inter può essere una trappola per almeno tre motivi: 1) conosce il valore dell'avversario e la rabbia che ha dentro per l'ultimo pareggio contro il Verona (3-3) e per una classifica non bella; 2) pur avendo avuto recenti e importanti segnali di maturità contro le medio-piccole, non si fida mai del tutto quando la sua formazione affronta un'avversaria sulla carta inferiore; 3) conosce la tradizione non positiva che i nerazzurri hanno contro i bianconeri. In particolare prima e dopo le festività natalizie.

Gli interisti si ricordano ancora il 16 dicembre 2017: Icardi e compagni erano primi in classifica e, in un San Siro colorato a festa, con tanto di canzoncina di Natale intonata dall'intero stadio, sfidavano i bianconeri. Doveva essere un pomeriggio di festa e invece finì con il successo degli uomini di Oddo su quelli di Spalletti (3-1). Da quel momento iniziò la crisi che però non impedì, anche grazie alla vittoria al ritorno a Udine (4-0), di conquistare il quarto posto e il ritorno in Champions dopo 6 anni di attesa. Sfogliando all'indietro le pagine della storia interista si arriva al 7 dicembre 2014, con Roberto Mancini da poco tornato in panchina per sostituire Walter Mazzarri.

Il gol di Isla a San Siro nel 2011. Lapresse

L'Udinese vinse al Meazza per 2-1 con reti di un certo Bruno Fernandes (dal 2020 allo United) e Thereau. I sogni di rimonta del Mancio vennero così drasticamente ridimensionati. Chi sedeva sulla panchina dell'Udinese? Andrea Stramaccioni che, quando era all'Inter, non ha avuto buoni risultati negli incroci con i bianconeri. Il 6 gennaio 2013, dunque il giorno della Befana, i nerazzurri di Strama franarono al Friuli (3-0) complice la doppietta di Di Natale e il gol di Muriel, ma durissimo fu anche il ko del ritorno, un 5-2 all'ultima giornata a San Siro, che spazzò via gli ultimi dubbi sulla conferma di Stramaccioni. Al suo posto Moratti, sul punto di cedere la maggioranza a Thohir, volle Mazzarri e pure lui ha rispettato la tradizione negativa contro l'Udinese... a Natale e dintorni: il 9 gennaio 2014 Inter ko in Friuli con la rete di Maicosuel. Un ricordo negativo contro i friulani, però, ce l'hanno anche Leonardo, sconfitto 3-1 il 23 gennaio 2011 (prima amarezza da tecnico dell'Inter), e Ranieri, battuto 1-0 al Meazza dalla rete di Isla il 3 dicembre 2011.

Guarda tutte le partite di Serie A Enilive in esclusiva nei locali Sky! Trova il locale più vicino a te.

Al di là del passato, però, Inzaghi ricorda bene soprattutto quello che è successo un anno fa, per la precisione il 18 settembre 2022 quando i suoi uomini furono sconfitti per 3-1 da quelli di Sottil: avanti con Barella dopo 5', i nerazzurri affondarono complice l'autorete di Skriniar e i colpi di Bijol e Arslan. Terza sconfitta in sette giornate per una squadra che a fine stagione di battute d'arresto ne avrebbe collezionate 12, finendo a -18 dal Napoli campione d'Italia. Visti i precedenti, più o meno recenti, Simone sa bene che domani sera sarà vietato distrarsi. Tanto più con il Natale alle porte...

#195. Pioli: "Con l'Atalanta sempre partite dure. A Bennacer serve tempo, a Jovic intensità..."

📅 Dec 08, 2023  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Stefano Pioli non si sente il “cattivo della storia”, ma neanche il deus ex machina del Milan, l’eroe della situazione, stavolta senza maschera. A fine conferenza c’è chi ha rispolverato Batman, in particolare la frase de “Il Cavaliere Oscuro” in cui si dice: “O muori da eroe, o vivi tanto a lungo da diventare il cattivo”. Pioli ha sorriso, ha ribadito che gli piace il personaggio e poi ha fatto leva sulle responsabilità. “Non mi sento il capro espiatorio, né un supereroe e né una vittima. Alleno il Milan, quindi ho oneri e onori. Devo pensare al bene della squadra”. E quindi domani andrà a Bergamo per giocare contro l’Atalanta. Di fronte ci sarà il solito Gian Piero Gasperini, l’allenatore più affrontato in carriera: “Ci sfidiamo dai tempi delle giovanili di Bologna e Juve, in Primavera. Più di vent’anni fa. Non gli ruberei nulla perché ognuno ha le proprie idee, ma lo stimo molto”.

L’Atalanta è sempre un crocevia importante per la storia di Pioli. “Partite difficili, lotteranno fino alla fine per un posto in Champions come noi”. L’avversario da tenere d’occhio sarà uno: Charles De Ketelaere: “Lo sto seguendo molto, gioca in un ruolo più offensivo ed è partito bene. Doveva capire certe situazioni, è un giocatore di prospettiva, è da monitorare con attenzione”. Niente da fare per Leao, infine. Il portoghese non ancora smaltito l'infortunio e non sarà convocato. Così come Kjaer e Okafor, anche loro ai box.

Guarda tutte le partite di Serie A Enilive in esclusiva nei locali Sky! Trova il locale più vicino a te.

Fondamentale il rientro di Bennacer, tornato a giocare una manciata di minuti contro il Frosinone: “Ha bisogno di un po’ di tempo. Le assenze sono sempre un’opportunità per dimostrare il nostro valore. Riguardo a Ismail, posso dire che ha fatto un recupero incredibile, con grande costanza. Lo aspettiamo al 100% della forma, prestò sarà titolare”. Pioli ha parlato anche di Jovic, in rete nell’ultima gara. “Ha talento e qualità, ma deve lavorare sull’intensità. Gli serve quest’ultimo step per diventare un giocatore top”. Uno di quelli che di solito gioca sempre, come Rade Krunic, anche se nell’ultimo periodo il bosniaco ha faticato di più: “Il problema è la condizione. Ha sempre giocato bene, ma ora ha avuto un calo. Ci sono compagni che stanno meglio di lui”. Pioli chiude parlando delle ambizioni. “L’obiettivo minimo è il quarto posto, ma possiamo fare anche di più. Il calcio che facciamo ha radici profonde, conoscenze certe. Dobbiamo essere competitivi in Europa e in Serie A con le nostre idee”.

#95. Goggia: "Questo era l'obiettivo, ma non sono stata perfetta"

📅 Dec 08, 2023  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sorpresa e pronta a nuove sfide. Dopo il 23° successo di Coppa del Mondo in carriera Sofia Goggia: "Mi sento un po' sorpresa - ha dischiarato sul traguardo ai microfoni della Rai -, è sono scesa con il 7 e nel superG a St. Moritz non è mai facile, non hai riferimenti. Quando ho visto il vantaggio di 95/100 mi sono detta: boh, devo aspettare tutte le altre. In pista ho avuto delle sensazioni non buonissime, ho dovuto un po' reinventarmi, l'arte dell'improvvisazione diciamo. Ero sempre molto lunga, fuori linea, però penso di aver preso molto bene le rampe nei tre punti cruciali della pista e di aver fatto la differenza lì".

Dome hanno dimostrato le tre top 10 nei primi tre giganti di stagione, il lavoro in estate è servito. "Sono più una sciatrice da discipline veloci, ma non posso vivere solo di quelle - ha spiegato Sofia -. Non penso di averlo messo così tanto in pista oggi, perché in alcuni passaggi ero un po' inclinata, non sono stata pulita. Ma l'obiettivo era questo". E nella prima prova di velocità della stagione, dopo il doppio annullamento di Cervinia, ha già messo la freccia: "Era ora di cominciare con la velocità - riconosce Goggia -, le prove qui a St. Moritz sono andate abbastanza bene. Ma concorrenza è agguerrita, dovrò fare analisi con i tecnici e dare il massimo domani e dopodomani". In programma ci sono ancora una discesa e un superG.

Con il successo numero 23 aggancia Federica Brignone in testa alla classifica delle plurivincitrici azzurre in Coppa del Mondo. "Siamo diverse: lei più tecnica, io più velocista. Ben venga questa concorrenza, perché questo tiene vivo l'interesse sullo sci e in Italia ne abbiamo bisogno".

#83. SuperG a St. Moritz: impresa Goggia, è il successo numero 23

📅 Dec 08, 2023  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Primo obiettivo raggiunto. Sulla Corviglia che le ha regalato una medaglia mondiale in gigante e dove l'anno scorso è stata ancora protagonista (si fratturò una mano, il giorno dopo vinse la discesa) Sofia Goggia torna a vincere in superG. Non succedeva da quasi due anni, da Val d'Isere del 19 dicembre 2021, e questa volta vale di più: quasi un secondo di vantaggio sulla seconda classificata, l'austriaca Cornelia Huetter (a 95/100), oltre un secondo su Lara Gut (terza a 1"02). Un dominio costruito dopo un avvio controllato e un'accelerazione che nessun altra è riuscita a ripetere, prendendo anche alcuni rischi prima di lanciarsi in un finale irresistibile. E la certezza che dopo i progressi in gigante, Sofia si è ripresa anche il superG.

Sofia rilancia la sfida con Federica Brignone: ora ha eguagliato la compagna di squadra a 23 vittorie (6 superG, 17 discesa) in Coppa del Mondo. Per Fede, reduce dalla fantastica doppietta nei giganti di Mont Tremblant, una giornata con qualche rimpianto. Un errore nel finale, quando ha rischiato di uscire dal tracciato, l'ha frenata mentre era lanciata a salire sul podio. "Peccato, ero in corsa per il secondo posto. Oggi non mi sono sentita a spingere, non mi sentivo a mio agio. La corsa con la Goggia? Non mi interessa, voglio pensare a come scio, alla mia prestazione". Alla fine Brignone è quinta (a 1"21) alle spalle di Mikaela Shiffrin. E settima, a 1"77, c'è Marta Bassino. Sedicesimo posto per Laura Pirovano, a 2"79.

Non era facile in una giornata di visibilità scarsa per la lieve nevicata che ha disturbato le atlete. Caduta paurosa per Elena Curtoni: dopo aver segnato il miglior intermedio nella parte alta della Corviglia, l'azzurra è volata via a cento chilometri all'ora, tradita da una cunetta in una giornata di pessima visibilità. Elena è atterrata sulla schiena, è scivolata per decine di metri ed è finita nelle reti di protezione. Si è aperto l'air bag, Elena si è rialzata ed è tornata sugli sci, ma è parsa dolorante a un ginocchio.

Risultati superG St. Moritz: 1. Goggia 1'16"63; 2. Huetter (Aut) a 95/100; 3. Gut (Svi) a 1"02; 4. Shiffrin (Usa) a 1"08; 5. Brignone a 1"21; 6. Venier (Aut) a 1"58; 7. Bassino a 1"77; 8. Suter (Svi) a 1"91; 9. Mowinckel (Nor) a 2"07; 10. Puchner (Aut) a 2"27; 16. Pirovano a 2"79; 29. Melesi a 3"93; 35. Na. Delago a 4"55; 38. Runggaldier a 4"76; 44. Ni. Delago a 6"07. Ri. Curtoni.

#124. Al Khelaifi: "Juve benvenuta nell'Eca se molla quel progetto stupido". La replica: "Non interessati"

📅 Dec 08, 2023  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

L'aspettano a braccia aperte, ma prima bisogna rinunciare alla Super Lega, un progetto "stupido", secondo il presidente dell'Eca, Nasser Al Khelaifi che però spalanca le porte alla Juventus. E in attesa di una sentenza europea sui monopoli Uefa e Fifa, rimane lo stallo tra l'associazione dei club europei, guidata dal qatariota, succeduto proprio ad Andrea Agnelli, quando due anni fa quest'ultimo fece secessione, puntando tutto sul campionato privato d'élite, insieme a Real Marid e Barcellona, gli unici club rimasti a sognare un'altra Champions League.

Guarda tutte le partite di Serie A Enilive in esclusiva nei locali Sky! Trova il locale più vicino a te.

Per il presidente del Psg Al Khelaifi comunque non ci sono tabù, come ribadito ieri al termine della riunione dell'Eca: “Se smettono di fare quel che considero sia un progetto stupido saranno sempre benvenuti”. Un messaggio chiaro, dunque, per rientrare nella famiglia Eca che in stretta collaborazione con l'Uefa ha respinto l'assalto della Super Lega privata, finalizzata a creare una sorta di campionato transnazionale chiuso, dove solo le big avevano garanzie sportive e soprattutto economiche. Un piano orchestrato da Real Madrid e Juventus, con il supporto del Barcellona, le uniche società rimaste dopo che i tifosi costrinsero i principali club inglesi a ritirarsi, come altre squadre italiane, tra cui le milanesi. L'apertura di Al Khelaifi però non è stata colta dal club torinese: "La Juventus – ha fatto sapere il servizio stampa bianconero all'agenzia Reuters – non valuta la possibilità di tornare nell'Eca, per il momento". Almeno fino alla sentenza della Corte di giustizia europea che il 21 dicembre dovrà esprimersi proprio sui monopoli di Uefa e Fifa nell'organizzazione delle competizioni sportive.
